People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

I don’t think that was the issue… but I’d rather not get into it much.\n\nDoctor: Had you used any substances before the incident at the fair?\n\nPatient: I already said… I don’t think so. During those months when I was hardly sleeping and pushing the farm expansion day and night… that’s when the voices in the irrigation lines were there too. And I’d see warnings in the yield maps. It wasn’t just one time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely sad or hopeless for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve had rough stretches, sure… farming’ll do that to a person. But “extremely sad or hopeless” for a long time… I’m not sure I’d put it that way.\n\nDoctor: When you're feeling like the whole valley's opened up for you, do you ever do things that you later regret?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess. At the fair, during my talk, I got up on the tractor and started moving the controls around, trying to show everybody the pattern I’d figured out. People were yelling for me to stop, but I just… couldn’t make myself quit. That didn’t look good after.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely sad or hopeless for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know. I’ve had low spells, sure, but whether it was more than two weeks like that… I couldn’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do your periods of feeling extremely happy or irritable seem to follow any kind of pattern or cycle?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a cycle exactly. Some mornings I wake up and it feels like the whole valley’s opened up for me… I’m just grinning for no reason. I was giving a talk and got up on a tractor, started yanking the controls around to show some kind of “new pattern.” People were hollering at me to stop, but I… couldn’t seem to quit.\n\nDoctor: Had you used any alcohol or drugs before giving the talk at the fair?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know what to say about that. I don’t think that was the issue… but I’d rather not get into it much.\n\nDoctor: Had you used any substances before the incident at the fair?\n\nPatient: I already said… I don’t think so. Out by the north pasture mostly, around dusk. Voices in the irrigation lines, telling me which rows are poisoned or blessed. It wasn’t just that day, either… happens even when I’m not all worked up.\n\nDoctor: Have you recently experienced a period of time where you felt abnormally happy, excited, or irritable?\n\nPatient: Yeah… some mornings I wake up feeling like the whole valley’s opened up just for me. I’ll be grinning over nothing. But then if one of the hands questions me, or my wife says I need to slow down, I… I snap at them. Feels like they’re insulting me, or the family.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voices in the irrigation lines are real?\n\nPatient: I don’t know how else to take it. When I’m out by the north pasture, near dusk, I hear them clear as anything… coming through the irrigation lines. They tell me which rows are poisoned or blessed. And it’s not only when I’m wound up, either.\n\nDoctor: When you feel like the whole valley's opened up just for you, do you think you have special powers or abilities that others don't have?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. I get to thinking I’m the one farmer around here who can actually prove a whole new way to grow things… like the university folks and the lenders ought to be listening to me, not sitting there doubting me.\n\nDoctor: Do these feelings of the whole valley opening up and hearing voices happen all the time or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: They come and go, I guess. During those months when I was hardly sleeping and pushing the farm expansion day and night… that’s when the voices in the irrigation lines were there too. And I’d see warnings in the yield maps. It wasn’t just one time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely sad or hopeless for a long period of time?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. I’ve had rough stretches, sure… farming’ll do that to a person. People were yelling for me to stop, but I just… couldn’t make myself quit. That didn’t look good after.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt extremely sad or hopeless for more than two weeks at a time?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know. I’ve had low spells, sure, but whether it was more than two weeks like that… I couldn’t say for sure.\n\nDoctor: Do your periods of feeling extremely happy or irritable seem to follow any kind of pattern or cycle?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s a cycle exactly. Some mornings I wake up and it feels like the whole valley’s opened up for me… I’m just grinning for no reason. But then if one of the hands questions me, or my wife says to slow down, I can snap pretty quick. Like they’ve insulted me or something.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling like the whole valley's opened up for you usually last?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a lot of the year, I guess. Those keyed-up stretches, where I hardly sleep and just keep going… maybe ten months out of the last year, off and on.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your periods of feeling extremely happy or irritable happen at specific times of the year?\n\nPatient: I can’t say I’ve noticed it tied to a season or anything.
